"""Simple test to understand the core issue."""

import re

# Let's manually construct what should happen
# Based on the debug output, we should get 3 proper records

# Expected structure for 12 columns:
columns = [
    "AllocCPUS", "Elapsed", "JobID", "JobIDRaw", "MaxRSS", 
    "NNodes", "NTasks", "REQMEM", "State", "SubmitLine", 
    "Timelimit", "TotalCPU"
]

# What we want to parse:
# Record 1: JobID=59374177
# Record 2: JobID=59374177.interactive  
# Record 3: JobID=59374177.extern

# The issue is that SubmitLine contains newlines, so naive splitting fails

# Approach: Instead of deciding upfront which method to use,
# let's always use the global approach but be smart about newlines

stdout_with_multiline = (
    "^|^1^|^00:00:02^|^59374177^|^59374177^|^^|^1^|^^|^2097M^|^COMPLETED^|^srun  --interactive --pty bash -c echo hello &&\n"
    "echo goodbye^|^20:00:00^|^00:00.042\n"
    "^|^1^|^00:00:02^|^59374177.interactive^|^59374177.interactive^|^0^|^1^|^1^|^^|^COMPLETED^|^srun  --interactive --pty bash -c echo hello &&\n"
    "echo goodbye^|^^|^00:00.039\n"
    "^|^1^|^00:00:02^|^59374177.extern^|^59374177.extern^|^0^|^1^|^1^|^^|^COMPLETED^|^^|^^|^00:00.002\n"
)

print("Input:")
print(repr(stdout_with_multiline))
print()

# New approach - always global field parsing
sacct_split = re.compile(r"\^\|\^")
all_fields = sacct_split.split(stdout_with_multiline.strip())

# Remove empty fields that might result from leading/trailing delimiters
all_fields = [field for field in all_fields if field or field == ""]

print(f"Total fields: {len(all_fields)}")
for i, field in enumerate(all_fields):
    print(f"  {i:2}: {repr(field)}")

# Regroup fields into records of the correct size
columns_len = len(columns)
result = []
if columns_len > 0:  # avoid division by zero
    # Calculate how many complete records we can make
    num_complete_records = len(all_fields) // columns_len
    expected_fields = num_complete_records * columns_len
    
    # Only process complete records, ignore incomplete ones
    complete_fields = all_fields[:expected_fields]
    
    for i in range(0, len(complete_fields), columns_len):
        record_fields = complete_fields[i:i + columns_len]
        if len(record_fields) == columns_len:
            result.append(dict(zip(columns, record_fields)))

print(f"\nRecords parsed: {len(result)}")
for i, record in enumerate(result):
    print(f"Record {i}: JobID={record.get('JobID', 'MISSING')}")
    print(f"         JobIDRaw={record.get('JobIDRaw', 'MISSING')}")
    print(f"         SubmitLine={repr(record.get('SubmitLine', 'MISSING'))}")
    print(f"         State={record.get('State', 'MISSING')}")

# The problem might be that we have the wrong number of total fields
expected_total = len(result) * len(columns)
actual_total = len(all_fields)
print(f"\nExpected total fields: {expected_total}")
print(f"Actual total fields: {actual_total}")

if actual_total % len(columns) != 0:
    print("INCOMPLETE RECORDS!")
    remainder = actual_total % len(columns)
    print(f"Remainder fields: {remainder}")</arg_value><arg_key>old_str</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""Simple test to understand the core issue."""

import re

# Let's manually construct what should happen
# Based on the debug output, we should get 3 proper records

# Expected structure for 12 columns:
columns = [
    "AllocCPUS", "Elapsed", "JobID", "JobIDRaw", "MaxRSS", 
    "NNodes", "NTasks", "REQMEM", "State", "SubmitLine", 
    "Timelimit", "TotalCPU"
]

# What we want to parse:
# Record 1: JobID=59374177
# Record 2: JobID=59374177.interactive  
# Record 3: JobID=59374177.extern

# The issue is that SubmitLine contains newlines, so naive splitting fails

# Approach: Instead of deciding upfront which method to use,
# let's always use the global approach but be smart about newlines

stdout_with_multiline = (
    "^|^1^|^00:00:02^|^59374177^|^59374177^|^^|^1^|^^|^2097M^|^COMPLETED^|^srun  --interactive --pty bash -c echo hello &&\n"
    "echo goodbye^|^20:00:00^|^00:00.042\n"
    "^|^1^|^00:00:02^|^59374177.interactive^|^59374177.interactive^|^0^|^1^|^1^|^^|^COMPLETED^|^srun  --interactive --pty bash -c echo hello &&\n"
    "echo goodbye^|^^|^00:00.039\n"
    "^|^1^|^00:00:02^|^59374177.extern^|^59374177.extern^|^0^|^1^|^1^|^^|^COMPLETED^|^^|^^|^00:00.002\n"
)

print("Input:")
print(repr(stdout_with_multiline))
print()

# Global field approach

all_fields = sacct_split.split(stdout_with_multiline.strip())
all_fields = [field for field in all_fields if field or field == ""]

print(f"Total fields: {len(all_fields)}")
for i, field in enumerate(all_fields):
    print(f"  {i:2}: {repr(field)}")

# Regroup
record_size = len(columns)
records = []
for i in range(0, len(all_fields), record_size):
    record_fields = all_fields[i:i + record_size]
    if len(record_fields) == record_size:
        record = dict(zip(columns, record_fields))
        records.append(record)

print(f"\nRecords parsed: {len(records)}")
for i, record in enumerate(records):
    print(f"Record {i}: JobID={record.get('JobID', 'MISSING')}")

# The problem might be that we have the wrong number of total fields
expected_total = len(records) * record_size
actual_total = len(all_fields)
print(f"\nExpected total fields: {expected_total}")
print(f"Actual total fields: {actual_total}")

if actual_total % record_size != 0:
    print("INCOMPLETE RECORDS!")

The issue is that when we split on `^|^`, we're separating fields that were originally separated by `^|^\n`, so the `\n` becomes part of the next field.

I think I need to approach this differently. Let me look at the actual issue one more time and realize that maybe I should just detect when we have the multi-line problem and handle it specifically.

But first, let me check if there's a simpler fix. What if the issue is just that I should strip leading/trailing whitespace from fields?

Actually, let me look at what the OLD code was doing that worked. The old code:

1. Split lines on `^|^\n` 
2. For each line, split on `^|^`
3. Create dict from fields

The problem occurs when step 1 doesn't work because of embedded newlines. 

But what if I modify the approach to be: split on `^|^\n` but if that fails (detected by wrong field counts), then fall back to a more careful approach?

Let me try a cleaner hybrid approach:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/troycomi__reportseff__2.8/src/reportseff/db_inquirer.py</arg_value><arg_key>new_str</arg_key><arg_value>        # Handle multi-line fields that break normal record separation

        # Remove empty lines
        lines = [line for line in lines if line]
        
        sacct_split = re.compile(r"\^\|\^")
        columns_len = len(columns)

                # Remove empty fields from trailing delimiters
                fields = [f for f in fields if f or f == ""]
                if len(fields) == columns_len:

"""Debug traditional parsing approach."""

import re

# Test data from working test case
stdout = "c1j1^|^c2j1^|^\nc1j2^|^c2j2^|^\nc1j3^|^c2j3^|^\n"
columns = ["c1", "c2"]

print(repr(stdout))

# Traditional approach
sacct_line_split = re.compile(r"\^\|\^\n")
lines = sacct_line_split.split(stdout.strip())

print("Lines after split:")
for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    print(f"  {i}: {repr(line)}")

lines = [line for line in lines if line]
print(f"\nNon-empty lines: {len(lines)}")
for i, line in enumerate(lines):
    print(f"  {i}: {repr(line)}")

sacct_split = re.compile(r"\^\|\^")
columns_len = len(columns)

print(f"\nColumns: {columns}")
print(f"Expected fields per line: {columns_len}")

# Try to parse using traditional line-based approach
traditional_result = []
if columns_len > 0 and lines:
    valid_lines = []
    for i, line in enumerate(lines):
        fields = sacct_split.split(line)
        print(f"\nLine {i} split: {fields}")
        # Remove empty fields from leading/trailing delimiters
        fields = [f for f in fields if f or f == ""]
        print(f"  After filtering: {fields} (count: {len(fields)})")
        # Accept lines that have the right number of fields (allow for empty padding)
        if len(fields) == columns_len:
            valid_lines.append(fields)
            print(f"  VALID (exact match)")
        elif len(fields) == columns_len + 1 and fields[-1] == "":
            # Handle trailing delimiter that creates empty field
            valid_lines.append(fields[:-1])
            print(f"  VALID (removed trailing empty field)")
        else:
            print(f"  INVALID: expected {columns_len}, got {len(fields)}")
    
    # If all lines are valid, use traditional approach
    print(f"\nValid lines: {len(valid_lines)}, Total lines: {len(lines)}")
    if len(valid_lines) == len(lines):
        traditional_result = [dict(zip(columns, fields)) for fields in valid_lines]
        print("Using traditional result")
    else:
        print("NOT using traditional result")
